So on 2026-10-06 a small stand-in was installed on the emulator that answers the Car App Library's question with what the test sets (FINDINGS_LOG). With it: a connection and a disconnection outside a trip were each written within a second, also with MilO three minutes in the background; during a trip started with the button the trip controller's own lines were the only ones; a connection made during a trip and ended after it was written when it ended; one made and ended while MilO was not running left no line, and one still there at the next start was written as "First reading … so MilO did not see it connect". What that cannot show: what the real Android Auto reports and when, and what HyperOS lets through to a MilO that is idle. Device checks 260 to 264.
- **Every process start now writes one more line,** the watch's first reading, unless a trip is already being recorded by then. It is what shows that the watch is running, and what a connection made while MilO was closed is noticed by.
- **In the instant a trip starts or ends, a change can be written by both watches or by neither.** Whether a trip is being recorded is asked when a report arrives, and the trip service's watch begins and ends a moment after the trip controller has published the trip. The gap is milliseconds. At a process start that the truck caused, the watch's first reading can arrive before the trip is open: it is then written, and the trip service's own first reading follows it a moment later.
- **A line is dated when MilO hears of a change, which can be later than the change.** Android 14 may hold a broadcast back from an app whose process is cached in the background until that process is active again, and nothing is known about what HyperOS does with Android Auto's announcement to an idle MilO (device check 264).
- **The watch outside trips never asks again by itself.** The trip service makes the library ask Android Auto once a minute while it believes it connected, because a missed "disconnected" would hold a trip open. Outside a trip a missed announcement costs a line of the log, and nothing else.
- **It asks the Android Auto app once at every process start** (the Car App Library's own query), as the trip service does at every trip start. On a phone without Android Auto the answer is "not connected". **"Type 0" does not say that the Android Auto app answered.** The Car App Library (read from its code in 1.7.0, and again in 1.8.0-rc01 on 2026-10-06: the class is unchanged) hands on 0 when the answer is missing, empty or cannot be read, exactly as for a real "not connected", so a line with "type 0" is written either way and "CarConnection reports no type" is a wording MilO has but this library never causes. Only a line with "type 2" shows that the watch reaches the Android Auto app (device check 261).
- **The daily "nothing recorded" check has never run on the phone.** On an emulator (Android 16, 2026-10-06, with the days made by setting the emulator's clock and time zone): the alarm started a MilO whose process had been ended and the notification was posted; the tap opened Home, from a MilO that Android had to start again (it had been left on Settings) and from the Trips screen; with something typed on the edit screen, "Leave without saving?" came first, and both answers did what they say; a trip started with the button took the notification away at once, and the same trip, discarded for being too short, still counted as that day's trip; switched off, the alarm was gone from Android's list, and back when switched on; after a restart of the emulator the alarm was asked for again without MilO being opened. Whether HyperOS lets the alarm start MilO with Autostart off, whether it delivers it at all to an app it has put to sleep, and whether the notification makes a sound there are device checks NR-3 and NR-9 to NR-11. **The emulator runs were made before the review's two changes** (the second look before a notification, and the schedule followed): those rest on unit tests, beside a real trip controller for the first, and have run on no emulator and no phone (NR-15, NR-16).
- **The daily check can only speak if MilO runs.** It is meant for the day detection has quietly stopped, and the commonest reason for that on this phone is that HyperOS does not start MilO. If HyperOS does not deliver the check's alarm either, nothing is said until MilO is opened, by which time Shawn has seen for himself. The alarm names MilO's receiver directly, like the reminder's; nothing is known about what HyperOS does with it (NR-9 to NR-11).
- **A work day without a drive before the time set brings the notification, every such day.** The check asks only whether a trip was started, not whether the truck stood still. That holds also while MilO waits beside a truck that is connected and parked ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15): a wait is not a trip, so on a work day with no trip the notification comes then too, beside "Truck connected and parked". A unit test has the two together since the verification of 2026-10-06 (evening) (`NothingRecordedWithControllerTest`): the notification comes during the wait, and the trip that the truck's movement starts takes it away. On the phone the two together have not run. And a trip that the parked rule discarded beside a standing truck counts as a trip started today, so on such a day the check is silent. The text says that this is fine, and the cure is the time: set it to when the first drive of a day is normally over.
- **It asks whether a trip was started, not whether it came to anything.** A trip that was started and discarded for being under the minimum distance counts as that day's trip, so a day on which MilO started a trip and recorded nothing useful is silent. "MilO could not start this trip" and the driving alert are what speak then, if anything does.
- **The trips are found by their stored start.** A trip MilO recorded today whose start was then moved to another day on the edit screen is no longer found, so that day can bring the notification after all. The other way round is caught: a trip of another day that was moved into today does not count, because the start MilO recorded is what is judged.
- **Every asking writes a line,** so opening MilO ten times on one day is ten lines, and a start of MilO from its icon is two (one for the process start, one for MilO coming to the front). That is on purpose (step 11); the log's trimming keeps the newest 1,000 lines whatever happens.
- **Moving the time later after the day's notification takes that notification away, and it does not come back that day:** it was shown today. The same goes for switching the check off and on again.
- **The check wakes the phone once a day** (`RTC_WAKEUP`), for a second or two. The monthly reminder does not; that is the one way in which its alarm differs. Switched off, the check asks for no alarm at all.
- **The day is the phone's day.** "Today" and the time are read in the phone's time zone at the moment of the look, so a work day spent in another zone is checked by that zone's clock.
- **Android removes MilO's notifications when MilO has just been installed or updated.** Seen on a loaded emulator on 2026-10-06: the notification was posted five seconds after `adb install`, and Android cancelled it a second later (its reason code says "the package changed"), while MilO had already stored that the day's notification was shown. On the phone this needs an update installed on a work day after the time set, with no trip that day; the "shown" line is in the Log all the same (device check NR-13). The monthly reminder is open to the same.
- **The check's switch and time are not in an export file,** although they mean the same on any phone ([Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)). An import leaves them as this phone has them; Android's own backup carries them, with the rest of the settings file (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, `[DEBT]`).
- **The tap's request names no trip and no screen but Home.** Like the reminder's, it is a request to MilO's one activity that any app on the phone could send; all it can do is show Home.
- **The reminder has never been seen on the phone.** On an emulator (2026-10-06) the alarm woke a MilO whose process had been killed, the notification was posted and its tap opened the Report screen. Whether HyperOS lets an alarm start MilO with Autostart off, whether it delivers the alarm at all to an app it has put to sleep, and whether the notification makes a sound there (MIUI is reported to switch channel sounds off) are device checks 215 to 219. If the alarm never arrives, the reminder still comes whenever MilO is opened or woken for anything else.
- **The daily alarm is another thing that starts MilO once a day.** A process started by it does what every process start does: the reconcile, the pairing check, the catch-ups, the driving alert's request.
- **The reminder is about last month only.** A month that was never sent stops being reminded of when the next month ends; nothing lists the months that are still open, except the Trips screen, month by month.
- **The reminder does not know that a sent month has changed.** A month with a report is "submitted" for it, however its trips have changed since; the Trips card is what says that ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)).
- **A reminder set to a day late in the month is short-lived:** set to the 28th it is shown for three or four days and then moves on to the next month.
- **The reminder comes while MilO is open, too.** Opening MilO on the reminder day posts the notification a moment later, while the screen is being looked at. It is the same look as every other.
- **A tap on the reminder while the edit screen holds something unsaved** asks "Leave without saving?" first, like every way out of that screen. "Discard" then opens the report; "Keep editing" stays, and the tap is forgotten.
- **The reminder's tap is a request to MilO's one activity** that names a month. Any app on the phone could send the same request; all it can do is open the Report screen on a month.
- Entries are ordered by the time they describe. A crash or a kill is written at the next start but dated when it happened, so it appears further down than the lines around its writing.
- During the hour that is repeated when the clocks go back, two lines an hour apart show the same time. Their order is still right.
- If the event log cannot be written while the trip controller is handling a failure, the process crashes on purpose, which leaves a crash file. A failure must not vanish.
- An unreadable settings file is never reset: a silent reset would lose the truck pairing without a trace. It also no longer crashes the start (that was a crash at every start, with no way out but clearing the app's data, trips included). The start-up import logs an `ERROR` entry and carries on, and the exit records are not imported while the file stays unreadable. Whatever reads the settings next has to deal with the failure itself, and each one that exists does. The trip controller logs it once and runs with the defaults. The pairing check reports `FAILED` with the reason, in its `PAIRING` line. The setup checklist logs an `ERROR` line and shows its rows without the confirmations. The Bluetooth receiver cannot tell whose broadcast it is, so it asks for a reading of the truck in place of trusting the event; that reading comes back unknown for the same reason. The companion service acts on its callback all the same, because Android only calls it for MilO's own association.
- If neither the event log nor the crash folder can be written, start-up does crash: there is nowhere left to record the failure.

---

## Backup, export and import

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone (phase 4, part B, 2026-10-06). Android's backup is switched on with rules of MilO's own, and the Settings screen has "Export all data" and "Import data". All of it ran on an emulator (Android 16), **never on the phone**: whether HyperOS ever backs MilO up, what its file picker looks like, and a move to a real second phone are open. Device checks 232 to 259 · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-06

**Required behaviour**
- The main database (trips, submission status, settings, event log) is included in Android Auto Backup. Raw GPS points live in a second database file that stays out of cloud backup, because Auto Backup is capped at 25 MB and backs up nothing at all once that is exceeded.
- Manual export and import of all data to a file.

**The three copies, and what each depends on**

| Copy | Made by | Holds | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| Android's cloud backup | Android, by itself, about once a day when the phone is idle, charging and on Wi-Fi | The trips, the sent reports, the event log, the settings. **Not the raw GPS points** | Backup being switched on in the phone's settings, Shawn's Google account, MilO staying under 25 MB, and **the signing key**: Android restores a backup only into a build signed with the same key |
| A transfer to another phone | Android, when a new phone is set up from the old one | The same, **and the raw GPS points** | Both phones in hand, the signing key |
| An export file | Shawn, with "Export all data" | Every closed trip, every sent report, the settings that mean the same on another phone (all but three: the daily "nothing recorded" check's switch and time, and the parked limit, all added on 2026-10-06, are not in the file yet), and the raw GPS points unless he switches them off. Not the event log | Nothing but the file. It is the one copy that is still good when the key, the account or the phone is gone |

*Android's backup* (`res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml`, `platform/transfer/MiloBackupAgent`).
1. **What is taken** is decided by the rules file, with exclude lines only: Android takes everything of MilO's that is not excluded. So `milo.db` is never named there, and is taken together with the `-wal` file Room keeps beside it. A rule that named `milo.db` alone would leave that second file behind, and on an emulator every trip of a fresh install was in it (the main file was 4,096 bytes, the `-wal` file 716,912).
2. **Taken, in both cases:** `databases/milo.db` with `milo.db-wal`, and `files/datastore/settings.preferences_pb`, the settings.
3. **The raw GPS points** (`databases/points.db` with its three side files) are excluded from the cloud backup and included in a transfer to another phone. On the emulator MilO held a 28.7 MB points database and its cloud backup was 113,664 bytes.
4. **Never taken:** everything under `no_backup/` (crash files, MilO's copy of a chosen trip-start sound, the backup agent's notes, the safety copies, a file that is being imported) and the cache (report files, the log as a text file), both of which Android leaves out by itself; the `-shm` and `.lck` files beside each database; `files/profileInstalled`.
5. **Before the files are collected, each database is brought into one file.** The backup agent opens each database with SQLite itself (not through Room, so no migration runs and no table is touched) and moves the write-ahead log into the main file ("checkpoint, truncate"; `data/transfer/DatabaseSettling.kt`). What is copied is then one file that is whole by itself. On the emulator the backup of a MilO whose `milo.db` was 86,016 bytes with 1,660,392 bytes beside it in the log copied a `milo.db` of 110,592 bytes and an empty log.
6. **Android does not always end MilO before a backup.** The documentation says it does. On the emulator, `bmgr backupnow` with MilO alive ran the agent inside MilO's own process (the same process id), with Room's connections open beside it, and ended that process afterwards; with MilO not running it started a process for the backup. Step 5 is what makes the copy whole in the first case: once the log is empty nothing moves into the main file again before a thousand pages have been written, so the main file does not change in the moment the copy takes.
7. **A backup that is over 25 MB is written down.** Android then backs up nothing and tells nobody. The agent's `onQuotaExceeded` leaves a note, and the next start of MilO writes an `ERROR` line: "Android did not back MilO up: MilO's files are 30.1 MB and the limit is 25.0 MB. Nothing at all is backed up while MilO is over the limit, and the last backup that worked stays as it was. "Export all data" in Settings writes a copy that has no limit". Seen on the emulator with 30 MB of filler in `files/`.
8. **A backup that was collected is one line too:** a `PROCESS` line "Android collected MilO's data for a cloud backup: … Whether the copy arrived is Android's to say (Settings, Google, Backup)". It is the only sign inside MilO that Android backs it up at all. A backup that was refused for its size does not get this line.
9. **A backup Android makes by itself does not end a recording:** `backupInForeground` is left off, so Android's own schedule passes over an app with a foreground service. **A backup that is asked for does not look.** `adb shell bmgr backupnow` during a recording ended MilO's process on the emulator ("has died: fg FGS"); Android started the trip service again ten seconds later, and the trip's points have a gap of 16 seconds. Never run it during a trip. Whether "Back up now" in the phone's own Google settings takes the same path is not known, and HyperOS may not start the service again: do not press it during a trip either (device check 259 finds out, on a trip that does not matter).

*After a restore* (`platform/transfer/BackupAftermath`, at every process start).
1. Android restores when MilO is installed, before it first runs, in a process where MilO's own code is not set up. The agent's `onRestoreFinished` leaves a note; nothing else happens then.
2. **The first ordinary start deals with it.** The restored settings are those of another installation, and four things in them are not true here:
   - **The truck's companion association.** Its number is Android's, from the other phone. MilO asks Android which associations it holds here (`truckOnArrival`, a pure function). With none for the truck, **the truck's address and name are kept and the association's number is removed**, and the pairing check that follows reports "paired before, pair it again": Setup's row says "Work truck was paired, but Android no longer watches for it. Pair it again.", Home says "Setup needs attention", and the pairing screen offers the truck. MilO never believes it is paired because a file says so. If Android does hold an association for that truck here (a restore over an installation that was not removed), the truck stays paired.
   - **The confirmations of the setup checklist** (Autostart, Battery saver, Other permissions, the recents lock). They were Shawn's word about switches of the other phone, and an uninstall resets the same switches on this one. All four are removed, so Setup asks again.
   - **The chosen trip-start sound,** if its file is not here. It never is: the file is under `no_backup/`. The setting is removed, and the built-in sound plays.
   - **A wait beside the parked truck** ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15), if the backup was made during one. It was true of that moment and of nothing else, so it is removed in the same write as the confirmations; the log lines below do not name it. **One gap:** this clean-up runs on a background thread, and the trip controller's first look at storage is asked for in the same moment, so nothing orders the two. If the controller looks first and the backup held a wait less than three days old, it picks the wait up. On a freshly restored phone Bluetooth is not allowed yet, so the truck cannot be read and the wait carries on; the trip service is asked for and refused, and Home shows that a start failed, until the next trip or the next restart of MilO. Read from the code, the last step shown in a test; never seen, and logged as `[DEBT]` (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06 (evening)).
3. Everything else is left as restored: the trips, the sent reports, the event log, the report's four settings, the schedule, the two numbers, the reminder, the date of the last export, a report that waits for "Did you send it?".
4. **Two lines in the log,** dated when the restore happened: `PROCESS` "MilO's data was put back by Android, from a backup or from another phone: … Taken out of the settings, because it was only true of the installation they came from: the confirmations of the setup checklist, and the chosen trip-start sound, whose file is in no backup. Go through Setup again on this phone", and `PAIRING` "After the restore: the truck (AA:BB:…) is known by address and name only. Android on this phone holds no association for it, so it has to be paired again. The association from the other installation was not kept".
5. **After a cloud restore there are no raw GPS points.** Every trip has its times, distance, positions and addresses on its own row, and "Restore recorded values" works from the row too (ARCHITECTURE, section 6), so nothing on a screen or a report changes.
6. **A restored database is opened like any other.** One from an older build is taken through the migrations, step by step; there is no destructive fallback. Seen on the emulator: a version 4 file restored by Android became version 5 at the first start with all 251 trips equal. One from a newer build is not opened: MilO dies at its start ("A migration from 6 to 5 was required but not found"), the file is left with every row, and the newer build reads it.

*Export all data* (the Settings screen's last card; `platform/transfer/DataTransfer`, `data/transfer/`).
1. "Export all data" opens Android's file picker with the name `MilO-export-2026-10-06.json`. Shawn chooses where it goes: Downloads, Drive. MilO needs no permission for that one file.
2. **One JSON document** (`data/transfer/ExportFormat.kt` describes it): what it is and which version of the form (`"format": "milo-export"`, `"formatVersion": 1`), when and by which MilO it was written, a sentence for whoever opens it, **how many of each thing it holds**, the settings, every sent report, every closed trip, and the raw points as rows of seven numbers. One thing on a line, so it can be read and compared.
3. **Every closed trip:** finished, discarded and deleted, with every column of the table, so a trip that is edited keeps what MilO recorded, a deleted one can still be restored on the Trips screen, and a trip sorted by hand stays so. A unit test holds the file against the tables' own column lists (`app/schemas`): a column that is added to a table and not to the file fails the build.
4. **The settings that mean the same on another phone:** the truck's address and name, the grace period and the minimum trip distance, the sound switch, the schedule and the choice for trips outside it, the driving alert's switch, the report's four, the reminder's two. Not the association, the sound's file, the confirmations, or any of the small pieces of state.
5. **"Include the GPS points"** is a switch on the card, on each time the screen is opened. Off, the file says `"points": null`. On the emulator 250 trips with 415,860 points were a file of 32.9 MB, written in about two seconds; without the points, 177 KB.
6. **Not while a trip is being recorded:** the buttons wait and the card says why. A trip in progress has no end and no distance yet.
7. **The file is whole or it is not there.** If the writing fails, or a trip starts while it is written, the file is removed again (or, if the app that holds it will not remove it, the card says that it is not a complete export). The same if a trip has started by the time Save is pressed: the file picker has made the file by then, empty, and MilO removes it.
8. **"Last export: Oct 6, 2026 at 1:29 PM"** stands on the card, with "without the GPS points" where that applies.
9. **A raw point that is stored for no closed trip is passed over, counted and said.** There should be none: they are what is left when the two databases have come apart (the trips put back by Android over an installation that kept its points; an import that could not be put right). The file holds closed trips only and an import refuses a point of a trip the file does not hold, so such a point cannot be in it; and an export that stopped at one could never be made, nor the safety copy every import begins with. The card says in red "Left out of the file: 2,700 GPS points that are stored for no trip this phone holds. The Log screen says which.", and the log's line names the trip ids, the first ten of them. What the file says it holds is what it holds. Seen on the emulator.

*Import data.*
1. "Import data" opens the file picker. **The picked file is first copied into MilO** (`no_backup/import/`), so that what is checked is what is imported.
2. **The whole file is read and checked before anything is touched** (`readExport`, `ExportChecks.kt`): that it says it is an export, in a form this MilO reads; every trip, sent report, setting and point, part by part; and the file against itself: as many of each thing as it says, no id twice, no point of a trip it does not hold. The file is walked through one value at a time, so its size does not matter, and its layout does not either: a file another program saved again is read the same (tried on the emulator with one written by Python, keys in another order).
3. **What is refused, and what the card says:**
   - not an export at all: "That file is not an export of MilO's. Nothing was changed."
   - cut short, or something in it that cannot be (a trip that is still being recorded, a position off the earth, an id twice): "That file is damaged or incomplete, so it was not imported. Nothing was changed. The Log screen says what is wrong with it.", and the log names it: "… Trip 7: its status is "OPEN", and only a closed trip can be in an export".
   - **from a newer MilO:** "That file was written by a newer MilO (export format 2). This MilO reads export formats up to 1. Update MilO on this phone, then import the file again. Nothing was changed."
4. **Then the question,** "Replace everything on this phone?": what the file holds and when it was exported, that importing REPLACES this phone's trips and sent reports (by number) with all their GPS points and merges nothing, what becomes of the settings and the truck, and that a copy of what the phone holds is kept first. "Replace everything" or "Cancel"; a press beside it is Cancel.
5. **A safety copy first.** Everything the phone holds is written to `no_backup/safety_copies/MilO-before-import-<time>.json`, a whole export with every point. If that fails, nothing is replaced. After an import of a picked file the newest three are kept.
6. **Then, in this order** (`platform/transfer/ImportRun`): a note that the import begins (`no_backup/import/begun.txt`); the trips and the sent reports in one transaction of the main database, which also writes a line into the event log; the settings in one write; the raw points in one transaction of the points database; the note taken away. A failure in the transaction of the trips leaves the phone exactly as it was. A failure in a later step does not undo the ones before: the card and the log say what could not be done, and the same file can be imported again. If the points cannot be stored, the points of the trips that are gone are removed, so that no imported trip is ever left with another trip's track.
7. **Not while a trip is being recorded.** Asked when the file is picked, when the question is answered, and once more inside the transaction that replaces the trips. A trip the truck starts after that is a new row with a new id, and its points are not touched.
8. **The truck after an import** (`truckOnArrival`): a phone that is paired with its truck stays paired with it, whatever the file names. Otherwise the file's truck is stored by address and name, **without an association**, and the card says "The truck has to be paired again on this phone before a trip can start by itself."
9. **What this phone knows about itself stays:** the association, the sound, the confirmations, the event log. The report that waited for "Did you send it?" is forgotten: its trips were replaced.
10. **"Put that data back"** stands on the card under each safety copy there is, newest first, each with the day and time it was kept ("Before an import MilO kept a copy of what this phone held then, on Oct 6, 2026 at 3:54 PM."). It offers that copy like a picked file, with the same question, which begins "This is the copy MilO kept before an import, on …" and says what the copy holds. So after two wrong imports the copy from before the first can be put back at once.
11. **Putting a copy back never costs another copy.** MilO keeps a copy of what the phone holds first, as before any import. Then, once the phone holds all of the copy that was put back, that copy is removed (the question says so), and no other copy is: the rule of the newest three is not applied. Pressed for the newest copy again and again, the button goes back and forth between two states and the number of copies stays what it was; the copy of what the phone held before the first wrong import is never pushed out by it. A copy whose points could not be stored when it was put back is kept.
12. **An import that MilO's process is ended in the middle of is finished at the next start** (`platform/transfer/ImportResume`, called last in the start-up work of `MiloApplication`). The trips and the raw points are in two database files and are replaced one after the other, the points taking some ten seconds for a year of driving. A process that Android ends in between (MilO swiped away, an update, a reboot) has stored the file's trips and still holds the raw points of the trips that are gone, under the same trip ids, and can say nothing. The note of step 6 says it for it:
    - **Were the trips replaced?** The note is written before they are, so it cannot say. The line the event log gets in the transaction that replaces them can: it is in the same database file and is there exactly if they were. The note holds that line's time and text.
    - **Not replaced:** everything is as it was. The note, MilO's copy of the file and the safety copy of that import are removed; the card says "MilO was closed just as the last import began, before anything was replaced. Everything on this phone is as it was. Import the file again.", and the log has a `REPORT` line. Nothing is done to the raw points: a trip that started since may have an id the file uses.
    - **Replaced:** the steps after the trips are taken again from MilO's copy of the file, which is still there: the settings, the raw points, the lines in the log, and the rest of the app told. Each can be taken twice. The card shows "Replacing the data on this phone. This can take a minute…" while it does, then "MilO was closed in the middle of the last import. It finished the import when it started again." above the usual "Imported: …"; the log has "MilO was ended in the middle of an import, and has finished it at this start from its copy of the file …". Seen on the emulator: 54 trips and 5,400 points, an import of 47 trips and 405,187 points, `kill -9` three seconds after "Replace everything"; after the next start every trip and every point compared equal to the file, eleven seconds after the process started.
    - **Replaced, and the copy cannot be used** (it is gone or no longer reads as the export it was, or a start has tried it before): the raw points of the trips that are gone are removed and the card says "… The trips and the sent reports are the file's. Its GPS points are not stored, and its settings may not be either. Import the same file again to make it whole. …". Seen on the emulator with the copy removed by hand.
    - **It touches no trip,** and only raw points up to the highest trip id the import knew: a trip that started since has a higher id. So it runs also while a trip is being recorded, as the second half of an import always could.
    - **A start that is itself ended while it finishes is counted** in the note. The start after it only removes the points; after two, the import is left as it is and an `ERROR` line says so ("… two later starts could not finish it. It is left as it is …"). Whatever ended two processes in the middle of this must not go on ending every process that follows: the trip service runs in each of them.
    - **Every safety copy is kept by it,** also beyond three; the next import of a picked file makes room.
11. On the emulator an import of 250 trips and 415,860 points took about ten seconds, and the trips, the sent reports and every point compared equal to what was exported.

**Where the code lives**
- `res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml` and the `<application>` element of the manifest: what Android's backup takes.
- `platform/transfer/`: `MiloBackupAgent.kt` (what Android calls around a backup and after a restore), `BackupAftermath.kt` (the first start afterwards), `TruckArrival.kt` (`truckOnArrival`, the pairing decision, pure), `DataTransfer.kt` (export, the reading of a picked file, the question, and where each stands), `ImportRun.kt` (the steps of one import), `ImportResume.kt` (the start after an import that was cut off), `TransferStatus.kt` (what the screen is told), `TransferLogText.kt` (the log's lines), `TransferFailures.kt` (a failure written down and kept from the process), `PickedDocuments.kt` (the picked file through Android's content resolver), `TransferBuilding.kt` (how the two long-lived objects are made).
- `data/transfer/`: `ExportFormat.kt` (the document and its version), `ExportMapping.kt` (between the tables and the document), `ExportChecks.kt` (what is refused), `ExportWriter.kt`, `ExportReader.kt` and `JsonScanner.kt` (writing and reading it without holding it in memory), `TransferDaos.kt` (the only SQL that can empty a table), `DataExport.kt`, `DataImport.kt`, `TransferFiles.kt` (the copy of a picked file and the note beside it, the safety copies), `ImportBegun.kt` (that note), `BackupNotes.kt` (the agent's notes), `DatabaseSettling.kt` (each database into one file before a backup).
- `data/settings/TransferStorage.kt`: the settings that travel, the date of the last export, what an import writes and what a restore takes out.
- `feature/settings/`: `DataCard.kt`, `DataCardTexts.kt`, `DataUiState.kt`, `DataViewModel.kt`.
- `app/TransferObjects.kt` builds them; `app/MiloApplication.kt` runs the aftermath at every start, and has an import that was cut off finished.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../data/transfer/` (the round trip of every kind of trip, of the sent reports, the settings and the points; the file against the tables' columns; every rule of the checks; damaged, cut-short and wrong-version files; the scanner; the files MilO keeps), `.../data/settings/TransferStorageTest.kt`, `.../platform/transfer/` (`TruckArrivalTest.kt`, the pairing decision; `BackupAftermathTest.kt`; `DataExportingTest.kt` and `DataImportingTest.kt`, an export and an import from start to end on stand-ins; `ImportCutOffTest.kt` and `ImportCutOffLimitsTest.kt`, an import whose process is ended where the stand-ins say, and the starts after it; `PuttingBackTest.kt`, one copy after another put back; `TransferLogTextTest.kt`), `.../feature/settings/DataUiStateTest.kt` and `DataViewModelTest.kt`.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Never run on the phone, and a restore cannot be tried there:** it needs an uninstall, which deletes the trips. A restore is first met on the day the phone is replaced. Until then the export file is the copy that is known to work; make one before that day (device checks 235 and 250).
- **Android applies the rules again when it restores, and for a backup made on the same phone it applies the cloud rules.** On the emulator a backup made in transfer mode held `points.db`, and the restore at install on the same emulator left it out; an explicit restore through the same transport in transfer mode (`bmgr restore`) brought back all 83,172 points. So the points arrive only by a restore that Android itself treats as a move from another phone. Google's own phone-to-phone transfer has not been run.
- **The emulator's test transport refuses a transfer over 25 MB as well,** which a real transfer (limit 2 GB) does not. That is how the refused-for-size line was first seen: a 28.7 MB points database in transfer mode.
- **Xiaomi's own phone-clone tool is not Android's transfer.** If it copies MilO's data, MilO's backup agent is not called and MilO does not know that anything happened. The truck's row on Setup still reports the missing association (it asks Android at every start), but the checklist's confirmations would come along as if they were true. After any move to another phone: go through Setup again.
- **A cloud backup needs the same signing key.** A build signed with another key is refused the restore (ARCHITECTURE, section 10). The export file is not tied to the key.
- **A backup may never happen.** Android backs up only with backup switched on, an account set, the phone idle, charging and on Wi-Fi, and whether HyperOS lets it is not known. The `PROCESS` line of step 8 is how to tell.
- **Android ends MilO's process after it has collected a backup,** also when MilO was alive in the background. The next trigger starts it again.
- **The safety copies are inside MilO and go with it.** An uninstall removes them, and no backup or transfer carries them. They undo a wrong import on this phone; they are not a backup.
- **A safety copy is as large as an export with every point,** and three are kept: on a phone with a year of driving that is on the order of 100 MB under `no_backup/`. There can be more than three for a while: putting a copy back and finishing an import that was cut off remove none, and the next import of a picked file keeps the newest three again.
- **Two safety copies kept within the same minute read alike on the card:** it writes the day and the time to the minute. The question that follows the button says how many trips, sent reports and GPS points the copy holds.
- **Finishing an import that was cut off happens at a process start, and takes as long as the points did** (about ten seconds on the emulator for 405,187). For that time it holds the points database for writing, so the fixes of a trip that begins in the same seconds are stored when it is done, later than they arrived and unchanged. An import that is under way when a trip starts always did the same.
- **Not covered: a power cut.** The two databases keep a change safe from the end of a process, not from the phone losing power within seconds of it (SQLite's write-ahead log, as Room sets it up, like every other write MilO makes). A phone that dies at the very moment an import ends could still come back with trips and points that do not belong together. An export would then say "Left out of the file: …"; importing the file again puts it right.
- **Raw points that belong to no stored trip stay where they are.** An export and a safety copy pass over them and count them; nothing removes them but the next import, which replaces every point up to the highest trip id.
- **An import leaves a large log file beside the points database** (`points.db-wal`, as large as the imported points) until Android next collects a backup, which empties it. It holds no data that is not also in the database.
- **An import replaces; it never merges.** A trip recorded on this phone since the file was exported is gone from the lists after the import, and is in the safety copy.
- **The event log is neither exported nor replaced.** It is the record of this phone. It is in Android's backup, so after a restore it is the other installation's log, followed by the lines of this one.
- **A restore over an installation that is still there** (Android Studio's "restore app state", `bmgr restore`) replaces `milo.db` and leaves the `points.db` that was there: the points are then those of the trips before the restore. A restore at install has no such leftovers.
- **Ids in a file are capped at two thousand million** (`MAX_IMPORTED_ID`): the tables number upwards from the highest id they ever held, and one absurd id would leave none for the next trip.
- **A file with an accuracy or a speed that is not a number has it written as "none"**; a position that is not a number stops the export, with the points switched off as the way round. Neither has been seen.
- **`"formatVersion"` must be raised with any change to the document,** and every earlier version kept readable. A new column of `trips`, `sent_reports` or `raw_points` is such a change.
- **No new category in the event log:** the lines of an export and an import are `REPORT` lines, a restore's and a collected backup's are `PROCESS` and `PAIRING`, a refused backup's is `ERROR`. A build of phase 4, part A, installed over this one reads everything (seen on the emulator).

---

## Settings

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. The settings of every phase are stored and have a screen of ten cards: the truck, the report's four settings, the monthly reminder's two, the three numbers of the trip rules, the work schedule, the choice for trips outside it, the driving alert, the daily check's two, the trip-start sound, and backup, export and import. The screen ran on an emulator only · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-06

**Required behaviour**
- Truck: the paired device, with a way to change it.
- Trip: disconnect grace period (default 2 minutes), minimum trip distance (default 0.3 km), trip-start sound (bundled chirp or a chosen audio file).
- Schedule: tracked days and hours, and whether out-of-schedule trips are saved as Personal or ignored.
- Report: name, company, vehicle description, accounts email. There is no rate-per-km setting.
- Reminder: on or off, and the day of the month.
- Daily "nothing recorded" check (not in the brief; Shawn's decision of 2026-10-06): on or off, on by default, and the time, noon by default.

**How it works today**
*The screen* (`feature/settings/`), opened with the square button with three sliders at the top of the home screen. It is not in the bottom bar; while it is open the bar keeps Home marked, and its Back arrow (or Back) leads to Home. (Opened with "Open Settings" on the Report screen, it stands on top of that screen: the bar keeps Trips marked, and Back leads back to the report.) Ten cards: Truck, Report for the accountant, Monthly reminder, Trips, Work schedule, Trips outside the work hours, Driving alert, Daily check, Trip-start sound, and Backup, export and import.
1. **Truck.** The truck's name as the phone gave it ("The truck" if it has none), and a button "Change truck" that opens the pairing screen (see [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)). With no truck stored: "No truck is paired", and the button says "Pair truck". Back from the pairing screen leads back to Settings. The card shows the name only; whether Android is still watching for the truck is the Setup screen's and the pairing screen's to say.
2. **Report for the accountant** (since 2026-10-06; what the report is, is under [Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)). Second on the screen, and not last, because the Report screen sends Shawn here for it. A few lines on what the four are for, then four fields to type in:
   - **Your name**, **Company** and **Vehicle** (under it: "The vehicle as accounts know it, such as its make, model and plate."). Each is stored as it is typed, without the spaces around it, 80 characters at most; an emptied field forgets the value. There is no Save button, as everywhere on this screen.
   - **Accountant's email address**, with the keyboard for an email address ("@" on it, no capitals, no corrections). **An address is stored only once it is one:** something before one "@", and after it a domain of at least two parts that ends in letters, with nothing else in the field. Until then the field is outlined in red and says under it "Not saved: this is not a complete email address. It has to look like name@example.com, with nothing else in the field." The address stored before stays in force until a whole new one is typed. An emptied field forgets the address. **The red always stands around the text it is about.** The Settings screen's ViewModel keeps the text that was refused, not only that something was, and a field that is built again while that ViewModel lives (the phone was turned, or the pairing screen was opened from Settings and closed) starts from that text, in red. It is never drawn around the stored address.
   - A report cannot be made without the name, or sent without the address. Company and vehicle can be left empty, and are then left off the report.
3. **Monthly reminder** (since 2026-10-06, phase 4; what the reminder does is under [Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders)). Directly under the report's own card, because it is the reminder to send that report. A switch, **"Remind me to send last month's report"**, on out of the box, and a quiet line under it: "A notification once a day, from the day below until the report for last month is recorded as sent. Tap it to open that report. A month without a Business trip brings no reminder." While the switch is on, a number between a minus and a plus button: **"Start on this day of the month"**, shown as "Day 1", from 1 to 31, with the line "In a month that has fewer days, the reminder starts on its last day." Switched off, the card shows the switch and the sentence only; the day is kept, and comes back with the switch. Each press is stored at once and has the reminder look again straight away, so a reminder that is showing goes the moment it is switched off or its day is moved past today.
4. **Trips.** At the top: "A change applies from the next trip. A trip that is being recorded may already use it." Then three numbers, each between a minus and a plus button:
   - **Wait after the truck disconnects** (the grace period): 0.5 to 10 minutes in half minutes, default 2. Shown as "2 min" or "2.5 min".
   - **End a trip after the truck has not moved for** (the parked limit, since 2026-10-06 (evening)): 5 to 30 minutes in steps of 5, default 10. Shown as "10 min". Under it: "The trip ends where and when the truck stopped, even if Bluetooth stays connected. A new trip starts when the truck moves again." Under 5 minutes a long red light or a level crossing would cut a drive in two; over 30, a truck parked at home with Bluetooth still connected records for most of an hour.
   - **Shortest trip that counts** (the minimum trip distance): 0.1 to 2.0 km in tenths, default 0.3. Shown as "0.3 km".
   A button is greyed out at its end of the range. Each press is stored at once; there is no Save. The two buttons do not move while the value between them changes (see `StepperRow` under [Design system](#design-system)), so a thumb can stay where it is and press again.
5. **Work schedule** (since 2026-10-06; what it is for is under [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal)). Two sentences on what the schedule does and when a change applies, then the seven days, Monday first:
   - Each day has a switch with its name. A day that is switched off shows nothing else; its hours are kept, and come back with the switch.
   - A day that is switched on shows two buttons side by side, "Start: 08:00" and "End: 16:30" on a phone set to 24 hours, "Start: 8:00 a.m." and "End: 4:30 p.m." on one that is not (the phone's own "Use 24-hour format" switch decides, as for a trip's times). Each opens the **time picker**: Android's clock dial under a title such as "Monday: start", with Cancel and OK. Nothing changes until OK; Cancel, Back and a tap beside the dial change nothing. The dial has AM and PM exactly when the times on the screen do: both go by the phone's switch.
   - **An end must be later than its start.** A time that would break that is not stored, and a red line says so **directly under that day's two times**: "A day's end must be later than its start. Nothing was changed. To move a whole day later, change its end first." If the line would come up below the edge of the screen, the screen moves up until all of it can be read; it does so for every time that is picked and refused, a second one for the same day included. It stays until the next press. (Until the review of 2026-10-06 the line stood once, at the top of the card, which for Friday is some 1,700 px above the button that was pressed.)
   - **"Use these hours on every tracked day"** is a button under a tracked day, shown only while another tracked day has other hours. Out of the box, with five equal days, there is none. Pressing it gives that day's start and end to every tracked day at once and asks no question; the buttons then disappear, because all tracked days agree. Days that are off are not switched on and keep their own hours.
6. **Trips outside the work hours.** One choice of two, each with a line saying what it means: **"Save as Personal"** (the default) and **"Ignore them"**. With "Ignore them" a trip is still recorded, then left out of the list and the totals; the card says where to find it ("on the Trips screen, switch on "Show deleted and discarded trips" to see one and count it").
7. **Driving alert** (since 2026-10-06; what it does is under [Safety net](#safety-net-manual-button-and-driving-alert)). One switch, **"Tell me when I drive without a trip"**, on out of the box, and two quiet sentences under it. The first says what the alert does and that MilO never starts a trip from it by itself, "the phone cannot tell the truck from any other vehicle". The second says that it needs the Physical activity permission, which the Setup screen asks for, and that the phone can take a minute or more to notice. The card does not show whether the permission is granted; the Setup screen's row does.
8. **Daily check** (since 2026-10-06, evening; what the check does is under [Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders), "The "nothing recorded" check"). Directly under the driving alert: both speak up when a trip is not being recorded. A switch, **"Tell me when no trip has been recorded"**, on out of the box, and a quiet sentence under it: "On a work day, if no trip has been recorded by the time below, MilO sends one notification. It catches the day MilO has quietly stopped noticing the truck. If the truck has not been driven that day, there is nothing to do." While the switch is on, a button **"Check at: 12:00"** (written as the phone writes times, so "12:00 PM" on a phone that is not set to 24 hours), which opens the time picker of the work schedule's card under the title "Daily check: time"; nothing changes until OK. Under it: "A work day is a day that is switched on in the work schedule above. If its hours start later than this time, MilO checks from their start. A trip you add by hand does not count." Switched off, the card shows the switch and the first sentence only; the time is kept, and comes back with the switch. Each press is stored at once and has the check ask for its alarm again and look straight away, so a notification that is showing goes the moment the check is switched off. **A press that could not be stored is said on this card,** under the time, in the screen's own words ("That change could not be saved. The Log screen says why."), and not at the top of the screen.
9. **Trip-start sound.** The switch, "Sound in use", Play, "Use my own sound" and "Use the built-in sound": see [Trip-start sound](#trip-start-sound).
10. **Backup, export and import** (since 2026-10-06, phase 4, part B; what each does is under [Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)). Last on the screen: it is used a few times a year, and it is the one card that can replace everything. From the top: what Android's own backup takes (the trips, the sent reports, the settings and the event log, with what the event log names: the truck and the other paired devices with their Bluetooth addresses, and addresses he typed) and that the truck has to be paired again after a restore; what an export is; **"Last export: Oct 6, 2026 at 1:29 PM"** (or "…, without the GPS points", or "No export has been made on this phone yet."); the switch **"Include the GPS points"**, on each time the screen is opened, with a line saying what they are, that the trips are complete without them, and that with the switch off the file still says where each trip started and ended, as an address and as a position; the button **"Export all data"**; a line on what an import does; the button **"Import data"**; and, once an import has been made on this phone, for each safety copy MilO holds, newest first, "Before an import MilO kept a copy of what this phone held then, on (date) at (time)." with a button **"Put that data back"** under it. While a trip is being recorded the buttons are greyed out and the card says "A trip is being recorded. Export and import wait until it has ended." While something is under way it says what ("Writing the file…", "Reading and checking the file…", "Replacing the data on this phone. This can take a minute…"), and the buttons wait. What the last export or import came to stands at the end of the card until the next one, one sentence each: with the tick what was done ("Exported: 250 trips, 4 sent reports and 415,860 GPS points."), with the empty ring what is left to do ("The truck has to be paired again on this phone …"), with the red exclamation mark what was not done. The screen moves so that those sentences can be read, once for each thing that changes there while the screen is open. Opened again with an outcome still standing, Settings starts at its top like any screen: the card draws nothing until its first real state is known (`DataViewModel.state` is null until then), so that what stands there from before is not taken for news. The card is shown also when the settings file cannot be read: an export then holds the trips without the settings and says so.
11. **When a change takes effect.** Nothing tells the trip engine. The trip controller reads the grace period, the parked limit and the minimum distance at every trigger, and the trip service reads the sound settings at every trip start, so the stored value is the one in force from the next event on, with no restart. For a trip that is being recorded this means: a grace period that starts after the change uses the new length, one that is already running keeps its deadline, and the minimum distance that counts is the one stored when the trip ends. The schedule and the choice for trips outside it are read the same way and used at one moment only, the end of a trip: a trip is sorted by what is stored when it ends. No trip that is already saved changes when the schedule does. The driving alert reads the schedule as well, each time the phone reports driving, to keep quiet outside the work hours; it starts nothing. **The driving alert's switch, the reminder's card and the daily check's card are the settings that do tell something.** the alert has no trigger of its own to read it at, so a press has it ask the phone to report driving, or to stop, at once. The reminder is told for a like reason: one that is showing has to go when it is switched off, not at tomorrow's look. The daily check is told so that it asks for its alarm at the new time, or takes the alarm back, and looks again at once. **The daily check also follows the work schedule, and that needs no telling:** it watches the stored schedule itself, so a change on the schedule's card (a day switched on or off, a start or an end moved, the hours copied) has it ask for its alarm again and look, with nothing in the screen's ViewModel for it. The report's four settings are read when a report is made: the next PDF has the new name, and a report already sent is not changed.
12. **If the settings file cannot be read,** the screen says so in place of every card but the last ("MilO cannot read its settings, so they cannot be shown or changed here. Trips are still recorded, with the usual values."), and an `ERROR` line goes to the event log. If a change cannot be stored, the screen says "That change could not be saved. The Log screen says why."
13. What eight of the cards show is decided by one pure function, `settingsUiState`, from the stored settings. Two cards have a state and a ViewModel of their own beside the screen's, because the screen's is at its size limit and neither card shares anything with the others: the daily check's (`nothingRecordedCardState`, `NothingRecordedViewModel`) and the last one (`dataCardState`, `DataViewModel`). The ranges and the stepping are `SteppedChoice` in `data/settings/SettingChoices.kt` (the three trip numbers, and the reminder's day); what hours a day may have, and the copy, are `WorkSchedule` in `core/schedule/`.

*The store.* `SettingsStore` in `data/settings/` keeps the settings in a DataStore Preferences file and is the only way to read or write them. `settings` is a flow of `MiloSettings` that delivers the current values and every change; `current()` reads them once. A value never written reads as its default. Each setter refuses a value that cannot be right: a negative duration, distance or time, a blank address, name or URI, a report text that is blank, untrimmed or over 80 characters, and an accountant's address that is not one email address. The schedule's setter needs no check of its own: a `WorkSchedule` cannot hold a day that ends before it starts. "No truck name" and "no sound name" are passed as null. If the file cannot be read, every read throws: the store never replaces it with empty settings.

Stored now: the truck's Bluetooth address, name and companion association id; the grace period (default 120 s); the parked limit (default 600 s) and, while MilO waits beside a parked truck, when the wait began and the place the truck is parked at (`ParkedStorage.kt`; not a setting, like the hold-off; the place is the closed trip's end position, or its newest usable fix if it never moved); the minimum trip distance (default 300 m); the trip-start sound on or off (default on); an optional custom sound, as the place of MilO's copy of the file and the name of the file that was picked; and values that are not Shawn's to choose but must outlive the process: the hold-off after a manual end, stored as the time End was pressed; how far the process-exit records have been imported; and the time at which he confirmed each step of the setup checklist that MilO cannot read (`ConfirmedStep`: HyperOS Autostart, Battery saver, Other permissions, the recents lock). A step that is not stored is not confirmed. Since 2026-10-06 also the work schedule (for each day of the week whether it is tracked, and its start and end as minutes since midnight; default Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 16:30) and whether trips outside it are ignored (default no: they are saved as Personal). A day whose stored times make no sense, which no setter can write, reads with the default hours. Also since 2026-10-06, whether the driving alert is switched on (default yes), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the time the driving alert was last posted (none before the first alert). Since 2026-10-06 also the report's four settings (the name, the company, the vehicle and the accountant's email address; each absent until it is set), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the report that was handed to the email app and waits for "Did you send it?" (`ReportHandOver`: its period, number of trips, total and the time), written before the email app is opened and removed when he answers. Since 2026-10-06 (phase 4) also the monthly reminder: whether it is switched on (default yes), its day of the month (default 1; a stored number that is no day of a month reads as 1), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the month the reminder was last shown for and the day it was shown on (`ReminderShown`), written each time it is shown. Since phase 4, part B, one more of that kind: when the last export was written and whether the GPS points were in it (`LastExport`). Since 2026-10-06 (evening) also the daily "nothing recorded" check, three values read as one (`MiloSettings.nothingRecorded`, a `NothingRecordedStored`): whether it is switched on (default yes), the time of day from which a work day is checked (default 12:00; stored as whole minutes since midnight, and a stored number that is no minute of a day reads as noon), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the day its notification was last shown. Their setters are extension functions beside their keys, in `NothingRecordedStorage.kt`, like the last export's in `TransferStorage.kt`; the time's setter refuses a time with seconds in it. **Which of all these travel in an export is one type,** `TransferredSettings` in `data/settings/TransferStorage.kt`: the truck's address and name, the grace period and the minimum trip distance, the sound switch, the schedule and the choice for trips outside it, the driving alert's switch, the report's four and the reminder's two. Everything else is about one phone or one moment and stays behind, **with two exceptions that are gaps and not rules: the daily check's switch and time, and the parked limit, mean the same on any phone and are not in the type yet** (Edge cases).

Who reads and writes: the trip controller reads the grace period, the parked limit and the minimum trip distance at every trigger, and writes the wait beside a parked truck when it begins and ends. It reads the stored hold-off only when it picks the state up from storage (after a process start, or after it dropped what it held in memory); from then on it keeps the hold-off in memory and writes every change. The trip service reads the sound settings at each trip start. The Settings screen writes the grace period, the parked limit, the minimum distance, the sound switch, the schedule and the choice for trips outside it, and `OwnTripSound` writes the custom sound. The schedule and that choice are read by the trip controller with the other settings at every trigger and used only when a trip closes, and by the catch-up at process start. The driving alert reads its own switch, the schedule, whether a truck is stored and the time of its last alert each time the phone reports driving, and the switch again whenever it looks at what to ask of the phone; it writes the time of an alert when it posts one. The Settings screen writes the switch, and the setup checklist reads it for the Physical activity row. The report's four settings are written by the Settings screen and read by the Report screen each time it shows or makes a report; the waiting report is written and removed by the Report screen (`ReportRecords`). The monthly reminder reads its switch, its day and the record of when it was last shown at every look, and writes that record when it shows one; the Settings screen writes the switch and the day. The daily check reads its switch, its time, the schedule and the day its notification was last shown at every look, and the first three again whenever it asks for its alarm; it writes that day when it shows the notification, and the card of the Settings screen writes the switch and the time. The truck's three values are written by `TruckPairing` (from the pairing screen, or when it adopts an association), and the confirmations by the setup checklist. Three more writes come from backup, export and import, all in `TransferStorage.kt`: the date of an export (`setLastExport`); the settings an import brought, in one write, with the truck stored without an association unless this phone's own truck is kept (`replaceTransferred`); and, after a restore, the removal of the association's number, the four confirmations, a stored wait beside the parked truck and a sound whose file is not here (`forgetOtherInstallation`). The truck's values are read by the Bluetooth receiver and the companion service at every event (to tell whether it is about the truck) and by every reading of the truck's connection.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/settings/`: `SettingsScreen.kt`, `SettingsCards.kt` (the truck, the three numbers, the driving alert's switch, the sound and the file picker), `ReportDetailsCard.kt` (the report's four fields), `ReminderCard.kt` (the monthly reminder's switch and day) and `ScheduleCards.kt` (the schedule, the time picker, the choice for trips outside the hours), `SettingsViewModel.kt`, `SettingsUiState.kt` (what the screen shows and the function that decides it). The daily check's card: `NothingRecordedCard.kt` and `NothingRecordedViewModel.kt` (with `nothingRecordedCardState`); its words are in `res/values/strings_nothing_recorded.xml`. The last card: `DataCard.kt` (with the two file pickers and the question before an import), `DataCardTexts.kt`, `DataUiState.kt`, `DataViewModel.kt`.
- `data/settings/`: `SettingsStore.kt`, `MiloSettings.kt` (the values and their defaults), `SettingChoices.kt` (the values the screen offers), `ScheduleStorage.kt` (the schedule's keys in the settings file), `ReportDetails.kt` (what a typed report setting is stored as, and what an email address must look like), `ReportHandOver.kt` (the report that waits for its answer, and its keys), `ReminderStorage.kt` (the monthly reminder's three values and their four keys), `NothingRecordedStorage.kt` (the daily check's three values, their three keys and their setters), `TransferStorage.kt` (the settings that travel in an export, the date of the last export and its two keys, and what an import and a restore write).
- `core/schedule/WorkSchedule.kt`: the schedule itself and its rules.
- `core/util/DurationFormat.kt`: `formatMinutes`, the "2.5" of "2.5 min". `core/util/TimeFormat.kt`: `formatClockTime` (a day's start and end) and `formatDayOfWeek`. `core/designsystem/component/TwentyFourHourClock.kt`: `rememberTwentyFourHourClock`, the phone's own 24-hour switch, which the two times and the time picker's dial go by.
- `app/MiloNavigation.kt` opens the screen from Home and the pairing screen from it. The two cards with a ViewModel of their own get it from `app/TransferObjects.kt` and `app/CheckObjects.kt`.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../data/settings/SettingsStoreTest.kt`, `ReportSettingsStoreTest.kt` (the four settings and the waiting report read back, their key names, a file with nonsense in it), `ReminderSettingsStoreTest.kt` (the same for the reminder's values), `ReportDetailsTest.kt` (which addresses are taken and which slips are caught), `ScheduleStorageTest.kt` (the schedule read back, its key names, a file with nonsense in it) and `SettingChoicesTest.kt` (the ranges, the steps, what each value looks like on the screen), `app/src/test/.../core/schedule/WorkScheduleTest.kt` (what hours a day may have, the copy), `app/src/test/.../feature/settings/SettingsUiStateTest.kt` (with which day a refusal stands under), `ReportSettingsUiStateTest.kt` (the report's four fields as stored, and that "not saved" stands with the refused address and never with the stored one) and `SettingsViewModelTest.kt` (the driving alert's switch: stored, then the alert told; the reminder's switch and day likewise, and the day's two ends), `DataUiStateTest.kt` (the last card: when its buttons wait, and which sentences stand for which outcome), `NothingRecordedViewModelTest.kt` (the daily check's card: what it shows, stored first and then the check told, a change that cannot be stored), `app/src/test/.../data/settings/NothingRecordedStorageTest.kt` (its three values read back, their key names, a file with nonsense in it), `app/src/test/.../data/settings/TransferStorageTest.kt` (what travels, what an import writes and leaves, what a restore takes out), `app/src/test/.../core/util/DurationFormatTest.kt` and `TimeFormatTest.kt` (with the phone's 24-hour switch against the language's own habit), and `app/src/test/.../app/MiloNavigationTest.kt` for the way in and out.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **The parked limit is not in the export file.** The grace period and the minimum distance travel with an export; the parked limit, added later, does not, because the file's form is fixed and versioned (`ExportFormat.kt`) and adding to it needs a new version with a reader for both. An import leaves the phone's own value in place. Android's backup restores the settings file whole, so there it does come along. Logged as `[DEBT]` in FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06 (evening).
- **The row "End a trip after the truck has not moved for" was seen on an emulator only** (2026-10-06: "10 min" out of the box, "5 min" after one press, and the minus greyed out there). Device check 265.
- **Never run on the phone.** Device checks 111 to 124 and 126, for the schedule 130 to 135, 151 and 152, for the driving alert's card DA-2, for the report's four settings 179 to 181, for the reminder's card 213 and 221, for the daily check's card NR-1 and NR-2, and for the last card 234 to 245. On an emulator on 2026-10-06 the screen was drawn, the two numbers were stepped, a trip recorded straight afterwards was discarded by the new minimum without a restart, and the screen came back after Android had killed MilO in the background (FINDINGS_LOG).
- The ranges (0.5 to 10 minutes for the grace period, 5 to 30 minutes for the parked limit, 0.1 to 2 km) were chosen while building and are Shawn's to change: three constants in `SettingChoices.kt`.
- The store accepts values the screen does not offer (a grace period of 0, for one). Such a value is shown as it is, and the first press of minus or plus brings it to the nearest value the screen does offer.
- There is no minimum distance of zero. With none, a start that never left the yard would count as a trip.
- A longer grace period keeps a trip open, and the phone recording, for longer after Shawn leaves the truck. The trip is still cut where the truck was found gone, so the walk is not counted.
- Changing a number is not written to the event log: each press would be a line. A trip's own lines show the values it ran with ("… is under the minimum of 300 m").
- **The switch "Include the GPS points" is not stored.** It is on whenever the screen is opened, so that an export is whole unless Shawn says otherwise that time.
- **An export or an import is not stopped by leaving the screen,** and what it came to is still on the card when the screen is opened again: both run outside the screen, and where they stand is kept by `DataTransfer`, not by the card. Ending MilO's process loses what the card said. An import that was under way then is finished at the next start, and the card says so ([Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)); a file whose question was never answered is removed at the next start.
- The screen has no "reset to defaults". The defaults are 2 minutes, 10 minutes of standing still and 0.3 km, Monday to Friday 08:00 to 16:30, "Save as Personal", the driving alert switched on, the monthly reminder switched on for the 1st, and the daily check switched on for 12:00.
- **The daily check is on out of the box,** by Shawn's decision of 2026-10-06, at noon. It says nothing until a work day has reached that time without a trip, and needs the notification permission the Setup screen asks for.
- **The daily check's time is picked on a clock dial, to the minute,** like a day's hours, and has no range of its own: any time of the day can be set. A time before a day's start is not refused; that day is then checked from its start, and the card says so.
- **The daily check's switch and time are not in an export file** (`TransferredSettings`), although the rule is that a setting which means the same on any phone travels. Adding them changes the form of the export file, which needs a new version of that form that still reads the old one; it was left for a change of its own. An import leaves the two as this phone has them (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, `[DEBT]`; tagged `TODO(debt)` in `NothingRecordedStorage.kt`).
- **A change of the daily check's card is written to the event log** as the check's own two lines: the alarm asked for (or taken back), and what the check then decided. **So is every change of the work schedule,** with "(the work schedule was changed)" in the two lines: a start and an end picked one after the other leave two pairs.
- **The daily check's card was drawn and used on an emulator** on 2026-10-06 (the switch, the time picker in its 12-hour form, the alarm following both), never on the phone.
- **The reminder's day is stepped a day at a time,** so from the 1st to the 15th is fourteen presses. It is set once; a list of thirty-one days to pick from would have been a new kind of control for it.
- **The reminder is on out of the box,** as the brief has it ("on the 1st of each month"). It shows nothing until last month has a Business trip, and needs the notification permission the Setup screen asks for.
- A change of the reminder's card is written to the event log only as what the reminder then decided (a `REPORT` line, when the decision is not the one last written).
- **The driving alert is on out of the box,** a judgement made while building: the brief asks for the alert, and it does nothing until the Physical activity permission is granted on the Setup screen and a truck is paired. A press of its switch is written to the event log (a `DRIVING` line), unlike the two numbers and the schedule: it changes what MilO asks of the phone.
- **A half-typed address is not stored, and is gone when the screen is left.** The field says so in red while it stands there. Turning the phone keeps it, in red. Leaving Settings with Back does not: coming back, the field shows the address that is stored, which can be the one from before, without red.
- **The check of the address catches slips, not wrong addresses.** A missing "@", a space, a comma for the dot, two addresses in one field are refused; `acounts@example.ca` is taken. Only the email bouncing shows that one.
- **With the keyboard open, the red line under the address field can be hidden by the keyboard** while the field itself, outlined in red, stays in view (seen on an emulator, 2026-10-06). Putting the keyboard away shows the line.
- A name is stored at every keystroke, so a name that is half typed when MilO is ended is stored half typed.
- **To move a day's hours past its present end, the end is changed first** (and to move them before its present start, the start first): each time is checked against the other as it stands. The red line says so.
- **The copy asks no question** and replaces the hours of every other tracked day in one press. It is undone by setting the days again, which is why the button is only shown while it would change something.
- A change of the schedule is not written to the event log, like the two numbers. Each trip's closing line says which hours it was judged by.
- The time picker cannot choose seconds, and the schedule stores whole minutes. The latest end a day can have is 23:59; a trip that starts in the last minute of such a day is Personal.
- The day names follow the phone's language. **Whether a time has 24 hours or AM and PM follows the phone's own "Use 24-hour format" switch:** the dial, the two times beside it, and every trip's times on Home and Trips, so that they agree with each other and with the phone's clock. Until the review of 2026-10-06 the language alone decided, and on a phone set to 24 hours in English the dial had AM and PM, where one missed tap stores a time that is valid and wrong. Where the phone's switch and the language's own habit disagree, the time is written plainly, "16:30" or "4:30 PM", and not in the language's own short form. Seen on an emulator, never on the phone (device check 152).
- **"That change could not be saved" still stands at the top of the screen,** above the first card, whichever card the press was in. For a press far down the screen it is out of view, the fault the schedule's own red line had until the review of 2026-10-06. It needs a settings file that cannot be written, and was left for a change of its own.
- **The time picker is Material 3's, which that library still marks as experimental** in the release the build pins (1.4.0). It is used through one component (`TimeDialog`), so a change to it in a later release is one file's repair; the weekly update PR would fail to compile, not misbehave.

---

## Design system

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. The look described here (the "Bento" design, 2026-10-06) has been drawn on an emulator only (Android 16): Home, Trips, Setup, Log, Settings, the form for a missed trip, and the three dialogs; with the emulator set to light and to dark, with the font size turned up, and with gesture and with three-button navigation. Two of the four status dots, the question mark and the empty ring, were on no screen there; they were drawn outside the app from the same numbers. After the review of 2026-10-06 the form for a missed trip, the calendar, the clock and a greyed-out stepper button were drawn there again, in the build with its fixes. Later the same day the Report screen was drawn there too, and one fault was seen and fixed: two choices in one tile that touched. Its "Period" tile, Settings' two choices and the form for a missed trip were drawn again in the build with that fix. The same evening the design's typeface, Sora, was put in. Each of the eight screens and the three dialogs was drawn there again with it; several of them also with the font size at 1.3 and at 2.0. One thing no longer fitted and was fixed: the calendar's list of years at the largest font size. Later still, Home was laid out as the design draws it, with the shared parts that needed: it was drawn there with no trip, while a trip was recorded, and at a font size of 1.3; the truck's tile in its connected look only in a throwaway copy of the build, because an emulator has no truck. Nothing of this look has been on the phone. Device checks NL-1 to NL-30 · **Surfaces:** Phone · **Last updated:** 2026-10-06

**What it does**
The one place colours, spacing, type and shapes are defined, and the shared components every phone screen is composed from. Check here before building any UI: reuse what exists, and add to it deliberately when something is missing (STANDARDS §8).

**The look (since 2026-10-06)**
- **It is the owner's design.** On 2026-10-06 Shawn supplied a design canvas with eight drawn screens in a direction called "Bento": a near-black page, on it rounded panels a shade lighter (called tiles here), one bright accent colour (a lime, a yellow-green), and no shadows. FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, has what was decided and why.
- **MilO is always dark.** It no longer follows the phone's light or dark setting: the design has no light variant, and one made up here would be a second design nobody drew. The window is painted in the page's colour from the first moment of a launch, so a launch does not flash white. The icons of Android's own two bars (the clock and battery at the top, the gesture line or three buttons at the bottom) are drawn light, always: with the phone set to light, Android would otherwise draw them dark on a dark page.
- **The foundation is everywhere; the design's layout is on Home only.** The colours, type, corners, spacing and every shared component are the design's on every screen. Home is laid out as the design draws it since 2026-10-06 (two tiles side by side, the large accent tile, the truck's tile). **The other screens still have the layout they had:** a column of tiles under a title. Their own layouts (Trips' month tile, the Log's coloured tags, Setup's count, the Report screen's two tiles) do not exist yet.
